Fails if\nthere are several parent revisions (i.e., the given revision is a merge).\n\nWith the `--from` and/or `--into` options, moves changes from/to the given revisions. If either is\nleft out, it defaults to the working-copy commit. For example, `jj squash --into @--` moves changes\nfrom the working-copy commit to the grandparent.\n\nIf, after moving changes out, the source revision is empty compared to its parent(s), and\n`--keep-emptied` is not set, it will be abandoned. Without `--interactive` or paths, the source\nrevision will always be empty.\n\nIf the source was abandoned and both the source and destination had a non-empty description, you\nwill be asked for the combined description. If either was empty, then the other one will be used.\n\nIf a working-copy commit gets abandoned, it will be given a new, empty commit. This is true in\ngeneral; it is not specific to this command.\n\nThe name \"squash\" comes from the idea of combining (squashing) the changes from multiple revisions\ntogether.\n\nEXPERIMENTAL FEATURES\n\nAn alternative squashing UI is available via the `-o`, `-A`, and `-B` options. Using any of these\noptions creates a new commit. They can be used together with one or more `--from` options (if no\n`--from` is specified, `--from @` is assumed).\n\nUsage: jj squash [OPTIONS] [FILESETS]...\n\nArguments:\n  [FILESETS]...\n          Move only changes to these paths (instead of all paths)\n\nOptions:\n  -r, --revision <REVSET>\n          Revision to squash into its parent (default: @). Incompatible with the experimental\n          `-o`/`-A`/`-B` options\n\n  -f, --from <REVSETS>\n          Revision(s) to squash from (default: @)\n\n  -t, --into <REVSET>\n          Revision to squash into (default: @)\n          \n          [alias: --to]\n\n  -o, --onto <REVSETS>\n          (Experimental) The revision(s) to use as parent for the new commit (can be repeated to\n          create a merge commit)\n          \n          [aliases: -d, --destination]\n\n  -A, --insert-after <REVSETS>\n          (Experimental) The revision(s) to insert the new commit after (can be repeated to create a\n          merge commit)\n          \n          [alias: --after]\n\n  -B, --insert-before <REVSETS>\n          (Experimental) The revision(s) to insert the new commit before (can be repeated to create\n          a merge commit)\n          \n          [alias: --before]\n\n  -m, --message <MESSAGE>\n          The description to use for squashed revision (don't open editor)\n\n  -u, --use-destination-message\n          Use the description of the destination revision and discard the description(s) of the\n          source revision(s)\n\n      --editor\n          Open an editor to edit the change description\n          \n          Forces an editor to open when using `--message` to allow the message to be edited\n          afterwards.\n\n  -i, --interactive\n          Interactively choose which parts to squash\n\n      --tool <NAME>\n          Specify diff editor to be used (implies --interactive)\n\n  -k, --keep-emptied\n          The source revision will not be abandoned\n\n  -h, --help\n          Print help (see a summary with '-h')\n\nGlobal Options:\n  -R, --repository <REPOSITORY>\n          Path to repository to operate on\n          \n          By default, Jujutsu searches for the closest .jj/ directory in an ancestor of the current\n          working directory.\n\n      --ignore-working-copy\n          Don't snapshot the working copy, and don't update it\n          \n          By default, Jujutsu snapshots the working copy at the beginning of every command. The\n          working copy is also updated at the end of the command, if the command modified the\n          working-copy commit (`@`). If you want to avoid snapshotting the working copy and instead\n          see a possibly stale working-copy commit, you can use `--ignore-working-copy`. This may be\n          useful e.g. in a command prompt, especially if you have another process that commits the\n          working copy.\n          \n          Loading the repository at a specific operation with `--at-operation` implies\n          `--ignore-working-copy`.\n\n      --no-integrate-operation\n          Run the command as usual but don't integrate any operations\n          \n          When this option is given, the operations will still be created as usual but they will not\n          be integrated to the operation log. The working copy will also not be updated.\n          \n          The command will print the resulting operation ID. You can pass that to e.g. `jj --at-op`\n          to inspect the resulting repo state, or you can pass it to `jj op restore` to restore the\n          repo to that state. You can also pass the ID to `jj op integrate` to integrate the\n          operation.\n          \n          Note that this does *not* prevent side effects outside the repo. For example, `jj git push\n          --no-integrate-operation` will still perform the push.\n\n      --ignore-immutable\n          Allow rewriting immutable commits\n          \n          By default, Jujutsu prevents rewriting commits in the configured set of immutable commits.\n          This option disables that check and lets you rewrite any commit but the root commit.\n          \n          This option only affects the check. It does not affect the `immutable_heads()` revset or\n          the `immutable` template keyword.\n\n      --at-operation <AT_OPERATION>\n          Operation to load the repo at\n          \n          Operation to load the repo at. By default, Jujutsu loads the repo at the most recent\n          operation, or at the merge of the divergent operations if any.\n          \n          You can use `--at-op=<operation ID>` to see what the repo looked like at an earlier\n          operation. For example `jj --at-op=<operation ID> st` will show you what `jj st` would\n          have shown you when the given operation had just finished. `--at-op=@` is pretty much the\n          same as the default except that divergent operations will never be merged.\n          \n          Use `jj op log` to find the operation ID you want. Any unambiguous prefix of the operation\n          ID is enough.\n          \n          When loading the repo at an earlier operation, the working copy will be ignored, as if\n          `--ignore-working-copy` had been specified.\n          \n          It is possible to run mutating commands when loading the repo at an earlier operation.\n          Doing that is equivalent to having run concurrent commands starting at the earlier\n          operation. There's rarely a reason to do that, but it is possible.\n          \n          [alias: --at-op]\n\n      --debug\n          Enable debug logging\n\n      --color <WHEN>\n          When to colorize output\n          \n          [possible values: always, never, debug, auto]\n\n      --quiet\n          Silence non-primary command output\n          \n          For example, `jj file list` will still list files, but it won't tell you if the working\n          copy was snapshotted or if descendants were rebased.\n          \n          Warnings and errors will still be printed.\n\n      --no-pager\n          Disable the pager\n\n      --config <NAME=VALUE>\n          Additional configuration options (can be repeated)\n          \n          The name should be specified as TOML dotted keys. The value should be specified as a TOML\n          expression.
